# 10 — Build log: decisions and changes since the plan

The plan is the state of `docs/00`–`docs/09`, `CLAUDE.md` and `PROMPTS.md` in the first commit (`5cb1005`, 2026-09-26). This file records everything decided after it: each choice a build session made where the plan was silent, ambiguous or wrong; each departure from the plan; each open question and what became of it; and every change to `PROMPTS.md`. Every session reads it with `docs/00-decisions.md` and adds its own entries before it ends. Cite entries as "per B-12".

Status words: **applied** (in the code, data or docs), **scheduled** (written into the named prompt, not yet done), **open** (needs the author).

## 1. The author's instructions during the build

| Date | Session | Instruction |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-26 | planning | Effort: high for every prompt, the maximum available for P-02, P-03 and P-05. |
| 2026-09-27 | P-02, P-03 | "push". The open questions of both reports were not answered one by one. |
| 2026-09-27 | P-04 | "go with defaults"; then "yes do it and push commits and change a bit in each coming prompt if needed but that doesn't mean settling for less." |
| 2026-09-27 | P-05 | "accept the defaults and push". |
| 2026-09-27 | P-06 | "accept the defaults and push, but make sure every post-start of building decision and change from the original plan that was made (that includes change of prompts) is written somewhere." (This file.) |
| 2026-09-27 | P-07 | "run P-07". |
| 2026-09-27 | P-08 | "run P-08"; midway, asked for a status ("it's already done? what's happening?"), then "yes continue". |
| 2026-09-28 | P-08 | "accept the defaults and push". |
| 2026-09-28 | P-09 onwards | Run the remaining prompts one after the other, each in a fresh session, with Opus 5.5, taking the recommended defaults; log every decision that differs from PROMPTS.md; commit and push after each prompt (unattended run). |

Standing rule taken from these: a session's recommended defaults are adopted, and follow-ups are written into the prompt that must act on them, without lowering any requirement. The P-02 and P-03 questions left unanswered are adopted under that rule in §2 below (2026-09-27, P-06). A default that changes a score, a cap, a threshold, the passivity rule or the universe is **not** applied by editing `methodology/v1.0.0`: it goes through a new version folder, a changelog entry and `diff.json` (`docs/08` §1, prompt P-24), and P-15 decides the set before scores are shown (P-22).

Since P-06 it lives at `apps/web/.env.production`, where Next.js reads it, not at the repository root as docs/04 §6 says. | D-16 gate must be in the repository; Next.js reads env files from the app folder. | Applied. |

| B-05 | Cloudflare values leave the local `.env`; only the two secrets in GitHub Actions. | CLAUDE.md: no secrets outside Actions. | Scheduled: P-12. |

Readings of docs/02 that could not be encoded literally:

| ID | Decision | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| B-07 | A6 is always a standing state; a suspension that ends gets its end date. | The schema allows one event type per indicator; docs/02 §3 made A6 standing only when open-ended. | Applied (A6 `notes`, CHANGELOG). |
| B-08 | B3, B5, B6 and C2 are standing (the final decision of docs/02 §3, whose earlier sentence called them repeatable). | docs/02 §3 contradicts itself. | Applied. docs/02 §3 now states the final list once (P-10, B-149). |
| B-09 | A1 has no data before 2024-03-11, the date of SIPRI's first post-war release (checked on sipri.org). | docs/02 only said "March 2024". | Applied (`thresholds.yaml` a1 `no_data_before`). |
| B-10 | A4 counts orders signed from 2023-10-07 as a parameter; orders dated 2023 are left out because SIPRI dates orders by year only (P-04 default). | The date cannot be checked from SIPRI data. | Applied. P-15 (2026-09-30): not measurable until the SIPRI import (B-440). |
| B-11 | A4 and C3 values hold from their source release to the next release. | docs/02 §5 is silent; the general rule of §3 for computed indicators. | Applied. |
| B-12 | A5 "confirmed instances only" defines an instance; no confidence restriction is added. | The schema has no per-indicator confidence field. | Applied. |
| B-13 | The scaled list (docs/02 §12.1: A5, B9, B12 tiers) is extended to every indicator whose points differ between events: A1, A2, A4, A5, B1, B8, B9, B11, B12, C1, C3, C4, D1, D3. | They need a `points_rationale`. | Applied (CHANGELOG). docs/02 §12.1 lists the 14 (P-10, B-149). |
| B-14 | The docs/02 §7 worked example is reproduced with s = 0.3025 and a vote 462 days old (d = 0.8007): −40·√0.30 gives −21.9, not −22, and d = 0.8 cannot occur with whole days. | Keep −13.6 / −14 (Passive) exact. | Applied in tests and the methodology page; docs/02 §7 text applied in P-10 (B-149). |
| B-15 | Quotes of B9/B10 are always checked against `archive/text`; other quotes are exempt only for dataset rows and `official-video` timestamps. | docs/02 §12.4 and docs/03 §4 differed. | Applied. |
| B-16 | B8 is generated (from the registry, then from `recognitions.csv`), though D-08 does not list it. | It is a table of dated recognitions. | Applied; `recognitions.csv` scheduled in P-14. |
| B-17 | B2: one fixed −20 event per veto, no indicator cap. | docs/02 gives no cap. | Applied. |
| B-18 | The "to be verified" examples of docs/02 (United States and Hungary B4 declarations) are not encoded. | Unverified. | Applied (left out). |
| B-19 | Three validator rules the docs do not list: events on or after 2023-10-07, dates in order (retrieval, review, correction, reply), no duplicate rows in structured tables. | Integrity. | Applied. |
| B-20 | `banned-words.txt` has 280 EN/FR entries; the methodology page tables are generated from the YAML (`pnpm methodology:render`). | The page cannot drift from the files. | Applied. |

### P-03 — Scoring engine

Readings of docs/02 (a five-reader audit found 63 items; these change scores or outputs):

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| B-36 | Worked example as in B-14. | Applied. |
| B-37 | Passivity, literal: an event's own p·w·d is compared with 2 before stacking; a standing event qualifies from its start date, so it stops qualifying 365 days after it began even while it holds; negative events qualify; scope must be `gaza`; the penalty applies from 2023-10-07. | Applied (rc.1). Questions in B-46–B-48. |
| B-38 | "Most severe" (B8, B12, C1, C4, D3) is chosen by points, not points × confidence weight; B5/B6 on the same day: the greater id wins. | Applied; the methodology page says it (P-10, B-140). |
| B-39 | Only `published` events score; `corrected` and `superseded` contribute 0 (see B-26). | Applied. |
| B-40 | The display integer is rounded from S in full precision, not from the one-decimal score (−20.46 shows score −20.5 and display −20). | Applied. |
| B-41 | Coverage: B2 is not applicable when no Security Council term overlaps 2023-10-07…t; A1 is no-data for everyone before 2024-03-11; a hand-set `has-events` without a published event becomes `unchecked`; `missing` = no-data + unchecked; the §14 example's ratio is 21/30 = 0.70, not 0.71. | Applied; §14 wording applied in P-10 (B-149). |
| B-42 | Sensitivity: ranks from full-precision scores, average ranks for ties; variants 3–5 rescore and can change who is passive; variant 3 moves only `reported`, not `disputed`. | Applied. |
| B-43 | Outputs: event counts leave out computed values; movers use the integer display score; permalinks carry `/en` or `/fr`; the version prints `1.0.0-rc.1`. | Applied. |
| B-44 | Engine guards: a standing or computed state dated before 2023-10-07 counts from 2023-10-07; two overlapping computed values of one indicator are refused. | Applied. |
| B-45 | `pnpm score --preview` scores drafts as if published (docs/06 §4 step 7 and P-D use it); `--list` lists one country's events. | Applied. |

Browser tests (axe WCAG 2.2 AA per kit row, 375 px with nothing reaching into the gutter, no-JS, CSP, redirect) run on the kit build, which contains the production pages. The gutter check was added after the first CI run on Linux found a 3 px overflow that macOS text metrics hid (CategoryRows now wraps its capped line on phones). | docs/05 §5 kit; no invented quote under a real source. | Applied; CI runs them. |
| B-89 | Weights: `?w=` written with plain commas (docs/02 §9 form); the weights panel is a `<details>` collapsed by default, rendered only once JavaScript runs (P-07: in the HTML from the start, hidden without scripting, B-105), in score mode only. `combine`, `userScore` and `bandFor` of @gai/scoring take only categories, clip and bands (`CombineModel`, type change only). | docs/05 §6; the browser recomputes S without the indicators. | Applied. |
| B-90 | CompareChart and CategoryDots in scorecard mode: one sentence, then a table of published events by category and country. | D-16: no score before the flip. | Applied. |
| B-91 | EventCard's evidence row names the publisher as the source link (title in the tooltip) instead of the literal word "Source"; the end date of a standing state sits on its own line; a line says why an event does not count at the build date; an ended state in the changes feed shows its points struck through, "no longer counted". | Legibility within docs/05's layout. | Applied. |
| B-92 | French terms follow methodology.fr.md: « bande » (band), « catégorie » (A–E), « mode fiche » (scorecard), « généré le », « non applicable »; French spacing is added to the messages automatically; colons live inside the messages. | Consistency (P-18 glossary). | Applied. |
| B-93 | `/` is `app/page.tsx` under a pass-through root layout (next-intl without middleware); the 404 page is bilingual; canonical and hreflang links are set per page (`lib/seo.ts`), never in the layout. | Static export; a layout's `alternates` would give every page the home page's canonical. | Applied. |

### P-07 — Home, Ranking and the map

| ID | Decision | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| B-95 | The API publishes `events.by_category` (A–E, adding up to `events.total`) on every scored country in countries.json and countries/{ISO3}.json, counted by `eventCategoryCounts` of @gai/scoring over the same events as `eventCounts`, with each indicator's category from the methodology. The site reads it; `lib/events.ts` is deleted (with its unused `signOf`). | P-07 prompt; B-82. | Applied (api.ts, build-data, API README, tests). |
| B-96 | "Download CSV" links two new dumps: `dumps/countries.csv` (every registry entry at the build date in ranking order: score, band, passivity, the clipped subtotals and passivity value in full precision so that a reader can apply other weights, coverage counts, events, last change) and `dumps/countries.scorecard.csv` (the same entries by ISO3 with nothing derived from the score: coverage, event counts by confidence and by category, latest event). The ranking page links the one of its mode; the file holds the published scores at the default weights, and the page says so. | No existing dump was the ranking table; the scorecard variant follows the monthly reports (D-16). | Applied (docs/04 §2, API README). |
| B-97 | Home in scorecard mode: the movers and the ranking strip, made of scores, are not shown; "Changed this week" takes the full width; a "Scorecards by country" section links the alphabetical list (`/ranking/#countries`). On phones the map is hidden (`hidden md:block`) and the ranking strip, below the search box, stands in for it; the DOM order is not changed to move it up. | D-16; docs/05 §5 WorldMap; reading order equal to visual order. | Applied. |
| B-98 | "Changed this week" lists the entries of `changes/latest.json` `recent` dated after `movers.d7.from` and on or before the build date (the movers' window), the first eight, with a link to the changes page; the count comes from `weeks`, which hold the whole window (`recent` stops at 20). With none: the count line ("No change dated in the seven days to …") and a link. | The heading states a fact only if the entries are from this week. | Applied. |
| B-99 | Page copy: home map heading "Countries by band, {date}" / "Research coverage by country, {date}"; ranking title "Ranking of N countries" / "Countries in the index" (scorecard); one sentence with the build date and the methodology version; excluded entities after the table (D-10). | docs/05 §6, §7 (headings state facts). | Applied (messages EN and FR). |
| B-100 | Two test builds: the kit build (`build:kit`, out-kit/) is built with `NEXT_PUBLIC_SHOW_SCORES=true`, so its production pages are the score-mode pages; out/ (`pnpm build`) is the scorecard mode as deployed. Playwright has one project for each (`score`, `scorecard`): the page smoke tests run in both, the kit tests in `score`. | Score mode could not be tested before the flip otherwise (D-16). | Applied (CI builds both before `test:e2e`). |
| B-101 | `postbuild` checks the JavaScript budget on every production page: framework = `rootMainFiles` + `polyfillFiles` of the build manifest, `noModule` scripts left out, gzip of each file; the table goes to `apps/web/.js-weights.txt`. It found `MastheadNav` (a client component) importing `switchLocalePath` from `lib/i18n.ts`, which shipped both message files in every page's layout chunk (4.8 KB gzipped, ranking page at 150.5 KB): the helper moved to `lib/locale-path.ts`, and `postbuild` fails if a client chunk contains a message file. Result: framework 128.4 KB; application code 14.5 KB on home (142.9 KB in total) and 17.3 KB on ranking (145.7 KB), 11.8 KB of which is next-intl's ICU formatter (next-intl's experimental message precompilation was not used: French spacing is applied to the message strings at runtime). | B-79. | Applied. |
| B-102 | The stylesheet is inlined into each page (`experimental.inlineCss`): on Lighthouse's slow 4G a render-blocking stylesheet costs a round trip of about 560 ms; LCP measured 1.35–1.66 s without it and 0.68–0.89 s with it. The per-page CSP meta lists the SHA-256 of each `<style>` in `style-src`; the header gains `style-src 'unsafe-inline'`, narrowed by the meta as for scripts; style attributes stay refused (the build fails on one, and hash-sources never allow them). Next.js 16 writes bundled assets' `url()` without the `/_next` prefix in inlined CSS, and its RSC payload repeats the stylesheet in length-prefixed rows that must not be edited (an edit broke hydration, React error 412, in this session's first attempt): the fonts are therefore copied to `public/fonts/` with the package version in the name (`scripts/fonts.ts`), and the build fails on any `url()` to a file missing from the output. The payload's copy adds about 12 KB gzipped of HTML per page. | docs/04 §3 LCP budget. Departs from B-85's "no inline styles"; the policy stays hash-only for pages. | Applied (docs/04 §3). |
| B-103 | Lighthouse CI (`apps/web/lighthouserc.cjs`, `pnpm --filter @gai/web lighthouse`, a CI step) on out/: 3 runs each of /en/, /fr/, /en/ranking/, /fr/ranking/, medians asserted: LCP ≤ 1500 ms, JavaScript transferred ≤ 150 KB, CLS ≤ 0.001, accessibility, best practices and SEO 100. "A mid-range phone" is Lighthouse's default mobile profile (Moto G Power, 4× CPU slowdown, slow 4G: 150 ms RTT, 1.6 Mbps), **applied** to the browser (`throttlingMethod: 'devtools'`), not simulated. Final build: LCP 0.68–0.89 s applied; the simulated estimate is 2.1–3.0 s, because on a local server every script and font arrives before the first paint and the simulation then charges them all to LCP, although a real slow connection paints before the asynchronous scripts arrive. CLS limit 0.001, not 0: the swap of the text face over its metric-matched fallback leaves sub-pixel movement of inline links (0.0001–0.0004 measured). | P-07 prompt; docs/04 §3. | Applied; method and CLS limit are open questions 1 and 2 of the P-07 report (defaults adopted). |
| B-104 | Metric-matched fallbacks for the text face: `Source Sans 3 Fallback` (Arial or Liberation Sans with `size-adjust` 92 % up to weight 500 and 88.9 % from 600, ascent and descent overrides from Source Sans 3's 1.024 and 0.400 em) and `Source Sans 3 Caps Fallback` (83.4 %) for the uppercase navigation; ratios measured in Chromium on the 580 text strings of the home, ranking and kit pages in both languages. The home page's shift went from 0.041 to 0; no element changes height when the font swaps, on the four pages at 412 px. Newsreader keeps its optical-size file (132 KB; the weight-only file, 58 KB, made no difference with applied throttling). | docs/04 §3 "no layout shift from fonts", with `swap` kept for text. | Applied (globals.css). |
| B-105 | The ranking filters and the weights panel are in the HTML from the start with a `.js-only` class hidden by `@media (scripting: none)`, instead of being rendered after hydration (P-06); the sort headers have the same 24 px box before and after hydration. Inserting them after hydration shifted the table (CLS 0.09). Browsers without the `scripting` media feature (before Chrome 120, Firefox 113, Safari 17) show the controls, inert, when JavaScript is off. | CLS budget; docs/05 §9 item 15 (no dead controls without JavaScript). | Applied (tests check them hidden without JavaScript). |
| B-106 | `_headers`: `/_next/static/*` and `/fonts/*` get `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` and lose the page-level headers (CSP, Permissions-Policy, Referrer-Policy, X-Frame-Options; `nosniff` stays) through Cloudflare's `! Header` lines, which `scripts/serve.ts` now applies too: they only concern documents and added about 600 bytes to each script response, which Lighthouse counts as JavaScript on the wire. `serve.ts` also gzips text responses, as the CDN does. | Budget measured on the wire; caching. | Applied; P-12 checked the `!` lines on Cloudflare Pages: honoured (B-169). |
| B-107 | A favicon, `app/icon.svg` (ink square, white serif "G", titled): every page logged a `/favicon.ico` 404, which fails Lighthouse's best-practices score. | A clean console. | Applied; open question 4 of the P-07 report. |
| B-108 | `mapCountries`, `searchCountries`, `rankingOrder`, `stripFrom` / `rankingStrip` and `changedThisWeek` (`lib/countries.ts`) are shared by the pages and the kit; the kit gains `Movers` and `RankingStrip` specimens (synthetic XAA–XAE). `Movers` shows the integer display delta and "from → to" per country, one layout for both directions. | One implementation per component (docs/05 §5). | Applied. |

### P-08 — Country page, share cards, citations

| ID | Decision | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| B-109 | Country page (`app/[locale]/country/[iso3]/page.tsx`, body in `components/CountryPage.tsx`), one per registry entry and language: title (name, region, ISO3, memberships held on the build date as plain text); a hairline, then the gauge, the coverage bar, the generated summary line (`summary` or `summary_scorecard`), the computed values in force (B-74: "Also counted at the build date: 3 computed values (A2, C3, D1)…") and the open leads; CategoryRows; Timeline; Events; "What was checked"; Replies; Cite, share, download; Compare with peers. Headings: one h1, an h2 per section (the gauge's is visually hidden), an h3 per event (the EventCard summary is now an `<h3>`, for heading navigation) and per reply. Region only, without the M49 subregion, which has no French name in the registry. The assessment table lists the 31 scored indicators (docs/05 §6), a note column giving, in words, why the build replaced the recorded status (published event, Security Council rule, a reason read from the structured tables), then the note and the searches. | docs/05 §6 Country. | Applied. |
| B-110 | Runs of computed values (`lib/event-list.ts`): the computed values of one indicator, by date, a value with `previous_points` null starting a new run. A run of two or more values is one entry of the list (`ComputedRun`), dated by its latest value: the latest summary, "36 computed values from …", the dates on which the points changed (with the value before), the latest value's row and evidence, then "All 36 values" (a table: from, until, points, basis, the table row linking to its line) and the archived responses the run cites, each marked "cited by k of n values" when not all cite it. The Timeline draws a computed value only when its points changed. Every value keeps its event id as the anchor of its table row; a link to it (Timeline, changes feed) opens the enclosing `<details>` by script (Chromium also does it natively) and clears a filter that would hide it. | P-08 prompt; 36 D1 months must not bury the hand-authored events. | Applied. |
| B-111 | Generated-event evidence in EventCard, same layout as other events: each `row N of data/structured/{table}` entry is read against the table's columns (`@gai/schema/structured`, a new export path for STRUCTURED_TABLES) and shown as a key/value list with a link to the line on GitHub at the build's commit (`manifest.git.sha`); the dataset responses the row cites are collapsed behind "n archived responses", each with its title, Wayback copy, hash (copy on click), id and retrieval date; a vote's press-release quote is shown as a quote. `points_rationale`, when present, is shown as "Basis: …" under the points on every event, hand-authored or generated (the same for all). | P-08 prompt; docs/05 §5. | Applied. |
| B-112 | Event filters (indicator, sign, confidence) are links. Without JavaScript each points at an empty anchor `#f-{facet}-{value}` placed before the list; app/globals.css hides the entries without the class `f-{value}` while that anchor is the `:target`, one rule per indicator, sign and confidence level of the methodology (a test fails if a rule is missing, so an indicator added by a methodology version needs its rule); one facet at a time. With JavaScript (`EventBrowser`) the same links set React state, the facets combine, the address carries `?indicator=&sign=&confidence=`, the current value is marked with `aria-current` (a thicker underline, no weight change, so no layout shift), and a count line, in the HTML from the start with `.js-only`, says "n of m shown". | P-08 prompt; B-105. | Applied. |
| B-113 | `?date=` snapshot (`DateSnapshot`): after hydration a valid date (YYYY-MM-DD in the window) fetches `scores/{date}.json` and redraws the gauge and the category rows under a banner ("As of 1 December 2023: score −5, Passive band. Coverage, events and the timeline below are as of the build date…", with a link to the current page); the summary line, which is of the build date, is not shown then; the coverage bar keeps its build-date line (B-70). Raw subtotals for the "capped" mark come from the series (one decimal), the clipped ones from the day file. An invalid or missing date gets a one-line notice. Scorecard mode shows no score: the banner says the page is the build-date scorecard. The redrawing views (`SnapshotView`) load with `import()` only for a dated link. To share them with the browser, ScoreGauge and CategoryRows became views taking a translator (`ScoreGaugeView`, `CategoryRowsView`, `lib/translate.ts`) with server wrappers of the old names; `pct` and `valuePercent` moved to a d3-free `lib/linear.ts`. The country page sends five more message namespaces to the browser (gauge, categories, snapshot, events, share) through a nested provider; other pages do not. | P-08 prompt; D-05 deviation 1. | Applied. |
| B-114 | Share cards (`scripts/cards.ts`, template `scripts/card-template.tsx`): satori then resvg, from countries.json and each country file, before `next build` in `build` and `build:kit` (the kit's cards in score mode); one per registry entry and language: `/cards/{ISO3}.png` (English, the path the prompt names) and `/cards/fr/{ISO3}.png` (French: a French page should not share an English card; a departure from the single path of the prompt and docs/04). Scorecard variant: "Scorecard · N events · N computed values · coverage X %" (computed values only when some are in force, B-74), gauge without colour or marker; excluded variant: "Not scored" and the reason. Fonts: static WOFF from `@fontsource/newsreader` and `@fontsource/source-sans-3` (new pinned dev dependencies; satori reads neither WOFF2 nor variable fonts) and `@fontsource/source-code-pro`. Two findings: satori writes one-sided borders as zero-radius arcs, which make resvg panic, so rules and outlines are drawn as filled boxes; resvg loads every system font on each render unless told not to (700 ms → 12 ms a card). `public/cards/` is not tracked. | P-08 prompt; docs/05 §5 ShareCard. | Applied (docs/04 §3, docs/05 §5). |
| B-115 | JSON-LD `Dataset` on every scored country page (name, description, canonical URL, language, CC BY 4.0, creator, methodology version, build date, temporal coverage, the citation of the page's mode, the two JSON distributions), `<` escaped; docs/04 planned it on /data only. | P-08 prompt. | Applied (docs/04 §3). |
| B-116 | Page metadata: title "Germany: −14, Passive" in score mode (docs/05 §7), "Germany" in scorecard mode, "Israel: not scored"; description the summary line of the mode, or the exclusion reason; Open Graph and a `summary_large_image` Twitter card with the language's share card; canonical and hreflang by `alternatesFor`. | docs/05 §7; P-08 prompt. | Applied. |
| B-117 | "Compare with peers": up to four scored countries of the same region and, in score mode, of the same band, nearest scores first (ties by ISO3); in scorecard mode, which has no score to measure distance with, the same subregion first, then ISO3; a "Compare these countries" link to `/compare/?c=…` (P-09). | docs/05 §6. | Applied; open question 3 (default adopted). |

| B-119 | `scripts/serve.ts` kept only the path when redirecting `/en/country/DEU` to `/en/country/DEU/`, which dropped the `?date=` of every dated permalink; it now keeps the query, as Cloudflare Pages is expected to (P-12 checks it). | Found by the snapshot test. | Applied; checked on Cloudflare Pages in P-12 (B-169). |
| B-120 | JavaScript: country page 17.3 KB of application code, 145.7 KB in total, 151,601 bytes on the wire (Lighthouse); it was 147.8 KB and 153,715 bytes, 115 bytes over, before the snapshot views were loaded on demand. The ranking page went from 17.3 to 18.0 KB (152,268 bytes on the wire, 1.3 KB under the 153,600 limit): the scoring package's formatting helpers are now imported by client components of two pages, so webpack no longer concatenates them into the ranking module and keeps their exports; measured against a build of the P-07 commit. Lighthouse medians with applied throttling: LCP 0.77–0.86 s on the seven pages, CLS ≤ 0.0004, accessibility, best practices and SEO 100. | B-79, B-101, B-103. | Applied; P-09 keeps the ranking page clear of new client imports (done: the ranking page imports none of P-09's components; 152,637 bytes on the wire after P-09, B-127). |

| B-122 | Tests: Playwright `country.spec.ts` in both projects (sections and heading order, every public event id on the page, canonical, hreflang, og:image and the PNG's size, JSON-LD, filters with and without JavaScript, a link into a run, `?date=` via the slashless permalink, the excluded page, axe with every `<details>` open, 375 px); kit tests of the five country views (structure, the fixture event's quotes, correction link and reply, axe); unit tests of the CSV row reader, the runs and filters, the filter CSS rules for every methodology value, and a rendered card of each kind. Lighthouse CI adds /en/country/DEU/, /fr/country/DEU/ and /en/country/ISR/. | P-08 acceptance. | Applied: 112 browser tests pass (4 score-only skips in scorecard mode), 52 site unit tests. |

### P-09 — Compare and Changes

Decisions marked "Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-28" were taken without the author, under §1's rule, where the prompt left a choice open or where the build departs from its text.

| ID | Decision | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| B-125 | Compare (`app/[locale]/compare/page.tsx`, `components/ComparePanel.tsx`, `CompareResults.tsx`): the server page has the title, one sentence, a `<noscript>` section ("The comparison needs JavaScript") listing every scored country's page, and the client panel in the HTML from the start (`.js-only`). The panel reads `?c=` once after hydration (`lib/compare.ts` `parseCompare`: codes upper-cased, in order, once each, scored countries only, at most five; excluded entities, unknown codes and a sixth country are named in one line, "Not compared: ISR, XXX"), fetches each `countries/{ISO3}.json`, and writes the choice back with `replaceState` (`?c=DEU,FRA`, plain commas). The picker is a combobox over the scored countries' names in both languages and ISO3 (`lib/search.ts`, shared with the home page's search box) with one chip per country (its line dash and dot shape, a link to its page, a Remove button). The charts, tables and citation load with `import()` once the first file is in (CompareResults), so the page's first JavaScript holds only the picker. Everything that changes after hydration or a fetch sits in `.compare-results`, which keeps `max(24rem, 70vh)` from the start so that the footer below does not move (CLS). Colours, dashes and shapes follow the order of the countries shown. | P-09 prompt; docs/05 §6 Compare; docs/04 §3 (the one page that fetches at runtime). | Applied. Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-28 (the prompt does not say how excluded or unknown codes in `?c=` are handled, nor where the no-JS fallback goes). |
| B-126 | `?w=` on Compare, score mode only: the lines are the series' change points re-scored with the reader's weights by @gai/scoring's `userScore` (the series gives clipped subtotals to one decimal and the passivity flag; the value is `passivity.points` when applied), with a line saying so and a "Show the published scores" button; the WeightSliders panel of the ranking page, collapsed unless the link carries weights; category dots and events are not weighted. A `w` that does not parse or equals the defaults leaves the address. Scorecard mode drops `w` (no score to weight, D-16). | P-09 prompt ("URL state ?c= and ?w="); docs/02 §9. | Applied. Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-28: the sliders on Compare are an addition (the prompt names only the URL state), the one-decimal subtotals a limit stated in the code. |
| B-127 | JavaScript. The three compare components became views that take a translator (`components/CompareViews.tsx`: `CompareChartView`, `CategoryDotsView`, `EventDiffView`), with server wrappers of the old names in `CompareChart.tsx` (the kit), as P-08 did for the gauge; the chart axes moved from Timeline.tsx to `ChartAxes.tsx`, which imports no message file; the country marks to `CompareMark.tsx`; the Compare page sends `compare` and `categories` through a nested provider (`compareClientMessages`). `lib/chart.ts` no longer uses d3-scale: its two linear maps are written out with d3's arithmetic (a test compared every day of the window and every tenth of a point with d3-scale before it was removed; `d3-scale` and `@types/d3-scale` left the dependencies), since d3-scale with d3-array, d3-format, d3-interpolate and d3-time would have weighed more in the browser than the chart; d3-shape (the step lines) is in the Compare results chunk only. `next.config.ts`: webpack's `splitChunks` considers only chunks loaded at page start. Without it, the modules the on-demand Compare results share with other pages (TooltipLayer, CiteThis, the formatting helpers; the weights code and WeightSliders) passed Next.js's 20 kB threshold and went into chunks of their own, which the country and ranking pages then loaded as one more request, without module concatenation: +1,325 and +791 bytes on the wire. Measured on the wire as Lighthouse counts it (script responses, headers included), P-08 → P-09: ranking 152,268 → 152,637 (963 bytes under the 153,600 limit); country 151,605 → 152,016; home 148,758 → 148,835; compare 150,159 (160,294 once a country's results load, outside the first-load budget); changes 148,707; a monthly report 146,878. Application code (postbuild, gzip): ranking 18.3 KB, country 17.7 KB, compare 15.9 KB, changes 14.4 KB, month pages 12.7 KB. | P-09 prompt (client versions without both message files; d3-shape in the Compare page's own chunk; the ranking page's margin). docs/05 §8 says the charts are drawn "with d3 scales": the scales are now two functions with d3's arithmetic. | Applied (docs/04 §3). Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-28 (departures: d3-scale removed; a Next.js webpack setting). |
| B-128 | "Cite" on Compare: `comparisonCitations` in @gai/scoring (citation.ts, with tests), the same three styles as a country page: "Gaza Accountability Index, Comparison: Germany −14 (Passive), France +3 (Acting), methodology v1.0.0, as of 26 September 2026, https://…/en/compare?c=DEU,FRA"; scorecard mode "Comparison: Germany, France (scorecards)". The link is not dated (Compare shows the build's data; the citation names the build date); it cites the published scores, never the reader's weights. | P-09 prompt ("Cite for the comparison"); D-16. | Applied. Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-28 (an undated compare link; a dated comparison needs `?date=` on Compare, not asked for). |
| B-129 | EventDiff lists the events that can score and, of computed values, only those whose points changed (`diffEvents`), as the Timeline and the changes feed do; each links to its card or run row on the country page. | 36 D1 months would fill the table; the P-09 rule for the feed. | Applied. Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-28. |
| B-130 | Changes (`app/[locale]/changes/page.tsx`): title "Changes from 24 August 2026 to 27 September 2026" (the weeks of `changes/latest.json`), one paragraph on what is listed, a "Built on {date} from the published data, methodology {v}" line, the feed ("No entry in 5 weeks" / "3 entries in 5 weeks"), then "Monthly reports, October 2023 to September 2026": a table of months, newest first, each linking its report, with the entries listed and the recomputations without change (from each month file's `counts`; "month in progress" beside the current one). The stale-build notice is a client component: the build date (`manifest.json` `build_date`, read at build) against the reader's clock, shown when more than three whole days (UTC) have passed ("This page was built on 27 September 2026, 5 days ago. The site is rebuilt every night: the nightly build has not run since."); the date is formatted on the server so that no formatting code ships. Without JavaScript the "Built on" line gives the date. | P-09 prompt; docs/05 §6 Changes. | Applied. Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-28 (the notice is computed in the browser, since a static page cannot know its own age). |

### P-11 — Embeddable widget

Decisions marked "Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-28" were taken without the author, under §1's rule, where the prompt left a choice open or where the build departs from its text.

| ID | Decision | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| B-153 | The widget is vanilla TypeScript in `apps/widget/src` (`config.ts`, `strings.ts`, `render.ts` pure string builders, `styles.ts`, `main.ts` the entry), built by Vite 8.3.1 (pinned devDependency, the version vitest already installed) into one minified IIFE, `dist/gai.js` (target ES2019, no source map, no global). It has no runtime dependency; it bundles `formatSigned`, `formatLongDate`, `roundHalfAwayFromZero` and `dayNumber` of `@gai/scoring`, so numbers (minus sign, French decimal comma) and dates are written as on the site. The P-01 stub test was replaced. docs/04 §1's "Preact or vanilla" is now "vanilla". | P-11 prompt ("no dependencies", < 15 KB); D-25 formatting shared with the site. | Applied. Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-28 (vanilla rather than Preact). |
| B-154 | "Respects Phase-1 mode": the site's build writes its config into the script in place of a placeholder (`apps/web/scripts/embed.ts`, run by `pnpm build`, `build:kit` and `dev` before `next build`): the mode read from `NEXT_PUBLIC_SHOW_SCORES` as the pages read it (`lib/mode.ts`), the window start, the score clip and the band segments of the methodology in force (`bandSegments`). The placeholder is parsed with `JSON.parse` at run time, so the minifier cannot fold it; a script no build configured shows its link only. The widget therefore makes one request (the country file) and follows the mode of the site that serves the script; with `data-origin` it reads a mirror's data but keeps the mode of the site that served the script. The copy is `apps/web/public/embed/v1/gai.js` (git-ignored); the build fails above 15 KB gzipped; `@gai/widget` is a devDependency of `@gai/web`, so turbo builds it first. | The country files carry scores in both modes (B-145); the embed page promises one request and the site's mode. | Applied. Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-28 (mode written at build, not read from a manifest field at run time). |
| B-155 | What the widget draws (docs/05 §5 in its own markup): a `<div>` inserted right after its `<script>` (so several sit on one page; scripts not yet handled are also picked up when `document.currentScript` is null), an open shadow root styled by a constructed stylesheet (not an inline style under a page CSP; a `<style>` element as fallback), `lang` on its root. First the link "Gaza Accountability Index: {ISO3}" to `{origin}/{lang}/country/{ISO3}/` (the site name alone and `/{lang}/` for an invalid code); it stays on a failed request, a file of another country or an unexpected file. ### P-13 — Country registry (195 entries)

Decisions marked "Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-28" were taken without the author, under §1's rule, where the prompt left a choice open or where the build departs from its text.

| ID | Decision | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| B-174 | CI's layout shift fixed without loosening the budget (B-171). Traced with the layout-shift API under Lighthouse's throttling (412 px, 4× CPU, slow 4G): on /en/ranking/ the filter buttons of RankTable sat in a wrapping row, so each button's place depended on the width of the labels before it, which changed when Source Sans 3 replaced its metric-matched fallback (0.00054 locally, 0.0012 on the runner's Liberation Sans); with 195 countries every membership filter appears and the French ranking shifted by 0.066 (score mode) and 0.123 (scorecard: a button wrapped to a new row and pushed the table down). Fixes: the filter buttons are a grid of fixed tracks (`.filter-grid`, `minmax(6.5rem, 1fr)`; a label longer than ten characters spans two tracks, decided from the label, never from its rendered width; `filterButton` in RankTable.tsx); the ranking's CSV note sits on its own line under the link; a Compare chip keeps the 36 px of its mark before the country file arrives (the name moved 44 px, 0.00059). The remaining shifts were text rewrapping when the text face swapped in: 27 % of the long text nodes of 13 pages broke lines differently in the fallback (a fallback split by character class, letters 94.9 %, digits 89.7 %, space 72.0 % at weight 400, still left 19 %), and a monthly report with 195 countries' D1 lines measured 0.0027. Every face is therefore `font-display: optional` (the display face already was): a face that has not arrived within the block period is not swapped in for that page view and is cached for the next; the metric-matched fallbacks stay for that case. A full Lighthouse CI run then failed only on /en/country/DEU/ (0.022, the three runs alike, not reproduced in isolation): the masthead navigation was re-laid out when the text face arrived inside the block period, after the text had been laid out invisibly in the fallback; every page therefore preloads the Latin text face (`lib/fonts.ts` `PRELOADED_FONTS`, 28.7 KB, React's `preload` in the locale layout; a test keeps the path equal to the stylesheet's), since Chrome holds the first paint up to about 100 ms for a preloaded optional font. The display face (132 KB) is not preloaded. After the fixes the layout-shift trace reads 0 on the 21 Lighthouse pages in both builds; the Lighthouse CI results are in B-183. | P-13 prompt ("fix it first, without loosening the budget"); docs/04 §3. | Applied (docs/04 §3). Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-28: departs from docs/04 §3 and B-104, which kept `swap` for the text faces; on a slow first visit the text shows in the metric-matched Arial or Liberation Sans. |
| B-175 | Registry sources and names. Codes, M49 numbers, regions and sub-regions from the English table of the UN M49 overview; the M49 sub-region, not the intermediate region, as the schema has one field (so Kenya's sub-region is Sub-Saharan Africa and Brazil's Latin America and the Caribbean). Names from the UNTERM country export (`term/downloadCountries` of the UNTERM API, the file the "download" button of unterm.un.org/unterm2/en/country serves; SHA-256 recorded in the file header): `name.en` and `name.fr` are the short names without the bracketed article ("United States of America", "États-Unis d'Amérique"), kept as UNTERM writes them, long forms included ("United Kingdom of Great Britain and Northern Ireland", "Netherlands (Kingdom of the)", "Türkiye", "Naoero" for Nauru, which M49 also uses), straight apostrophes as in UNTERM. `name.fr_def`: the French short name with the article UNTERM brackets ("l'Allemagne", "la France", "les États-Unis d'Amérique"; "Les Bahamas" with the capital UNTERM gives), the name alone where UNTERM gives none ("Cuba", "Malte", "Israël"), and for the five short names whose bracket is a descriptor, not an article (Bolivie, Iran, Micronésie, Pays-Bas, Venezuela), the UNTERM French formal name ("l'État plurinational de Bolivie", "le Royaume des Pays-Bas"). DEU, ISR and PSE keep their P-02 text. | P-13 prompt; docs/02 §1. | Applied. Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-28 (the formal name for the five descriptor names; "Les" capitalised; straight apostrophes; M49 sub-region rather than intermediate region). |

The MEA site (rebuilt in 2026) loads every text by script from `/FrontEnd/FetchPublicationDetailData?pkid=…`, and its listing endpoint `/FrontEnd/FetchPublicationListingData` takes a keyword (titles only): that listing served as the search facility (press releases, speeches, bilateral documents, briefings, answers in both Houses, 2023-10 to 2026-09), and the full texts of all 429 speeches and statements of the Prime Minister and the Minister of External Affairs listed since 7 October 2023 were read by script for "Gaza" and "Palestin". A Save Page Now capture of the detail endpoint returns an HTML fragment without `<body>`, from which the archiver extracts no text; that attempt's index row and empty text were removed (no record written), and the MEA documents are recorded from Wayback captures of the former server-rendered pages (`Speeches-Statements.htm?dtl/…`, `--capture`, B-188), which hold the full text. pmindia.gov.in answered the new snapshot with HTTP 403, so the Knesset address is recorded from its capture of 26 February 2026 (the failed row removed). pib.gov.in captures cleanly; its releases were found by reading release ids around the dates of the agreements (English titles only). Press leads come from the Wayback listings of thewire.in and the articles read directly. `pnpm fetch:comtrade --reporters IND` was run (B-194): 21 A2 and 8 C3 rows, 32 keyed calls of 500 that day; P-14's earlier partial IND records (`src_20260928_comtrade_ind-self-2022`, `-2024`, `-2025`) stay as orphans (warnings), the rows citing the new captures. | docs/06 §4 and §6; the P-D prompt (Comtrade missing for IND). | Applied. Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-28. |
| B-246 | Readings of the Indian evidence, flagged for the author: (1) **B10 for the Knesset address** (25 February 2026): "India stands with Israel, firmly, with full conviction, in this moment, and beyond", closing the condolences for the attack of 7 October, with no condition on conduct and no mention of international humanitarian law, read as unconditional support as Germany's statement of 12 October 2023 (B-209 (2)); without it the preview is −24.8 instead of −29.8 (still Enabling). | B-267 | Readings of the Australian evidence, flagged for the author: (1) **A3 standing, no end**, on the Department of Defence's release of 11 April 2025 (more than 75 Australian companies supply F-35 components, avionics and propulsion, and sustainment; over AUD 5 billion in contracts), a national ministry statement; A/HRC/59/23 does not name Australia; the Government's statements that no weapons are exported to Israel do not address components supplied to the global programme, so they do not end A3. Denmark's Comtrade rows existed (P-14), so `pnpm fetch:comtrade` was not run. The Curia Vista pages are rendered by script, so the Federal Council's answers were archived as the Parliament's OData records (Atom XML, `Business(ID=…,Language='DE'|'FR')`, captured with Save Page Now, text extracted raw with the answer's HTML escaped; quotes taken within one text run), filed as kind official (the Federal Council's words in the parliament's official record, as the Storting and Folketing answers). | B-314 | **Speakers for Hungary.** B10 counts the head of government only: Prime Minister Orbán's words of 3 January 2024 (Israel has "the right to do everything" so that such an attack does not recur) and 3 April 2025 (press statement with Prime Minister Netanyahu: Hungary stands by Israel's right to self-defence, "you can always count on us") are filed as B10 under the standard of B-308 (support stated without reference to humanitarian law or civilians in Gaza); his words of 13 October 2023 (Israel's right to take steps, with a call for de-escalation) are not. Statements of other ministers (the Minister heading the Prime Minister's Office, "we fully support Israel", 9 October 2023; the Foreign Minister's) are recorded in the assessment notes, not filed as B10. B9: the Foreign Minister's own quoted words in the Ministry's releases only (4 March 2024 and 11 August 2025, calls for aid to reach Gaza's civilians and for more humanitarian access; 23 January 2025, the ceasefire to work in full), each at the call tier, as India's and the ceasefire-implementation calls on the other wave branches; statements in indirect speech and the joint leaders' statement on the hostages of 25 April 2024 are not filed. | The P-D prompt; symmetric treatment across countries. | Applied. | B-315 | **Search and archiving routes for Hungary, and the base merge.** (1) `origin/data/structured` had one commit the wave branch lacked (ce00297, B-903, B-904): merged in with a merge commit, no conflict. (2) **No web search** (the run's 200 calls were used up, as B-303, B-309). Hungary changed government in May 2026 and the previous government's site is now served from `2020-2025.kormany.hu` (news to 10 November 2025 and the Prime Minister's transcripts; miniszterelnok.hu and abouthungary.hu answer "under reconstruction"); `kormany.hu` holds the news from October 2025 on, including the new Government's. Both were searched through their own search APIs (`/publicapi/global-search`, full text; `/application/search`), terms listed in the header of `data/assessments/HUN.yaml`: 1,580 items on the archive site for the Gaza terms (274 relevant from 7 October 2023, read in full), then the broad terms of the other indicators; 999, 53 and 918 items on the current site. (3) 19 sources archived: 16 kormany.hu pages captured anew (Save Page Now accepted them in about a minute each); the UN depositary notifications C.N.225.2025 and C.N.180.2026 recorded from existing Wayback captures (`--capture`, B-188: treaties.un.org answers Save Page Now with HTTP 403; the failed attempts' two empty index rows were removed before the commit); Hungary's declaration of intervention in case 192 archived from its UNISPAL copy (the ICJ's copy answers HTTP 403 and Wayback holds only a 403 capture of it). The declaration is a scanned PDF without a text layer, so the archived text is an OCR transcription (tesseract 5.5.2) of the archived bytes, marked as such at its top and in the source notes, and checked against the page images for every quoted passage. The ICJ press release of 13 March 2026 was copied byte for byte from `origin/data/wave-1` (record, text, index row), not archived again. (4) Hungary had Comtrade rows from P-14, so no fetch was run. No leads (no press or NGO claim was read). | docs/06 §4 and §6; the task's rule not to bypass limits. | Applied. Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-29. |

Three B6 events: the Prime Minister's statement of 22 November 2024 that the warrant would have "no effect in Hungary" (standing, no end); the official visit of Prime Minister Netanyahu of 2–6 April 2025 without arrest (standing from 2 April, no end); the withdrawal notification (effected 2 June 2025, ended 29 May 2026). Branch `data/wave-6` (base as B-330; `origin/data/structured` had no commits the branch lacked, so nothing was merged), same pull request as JPN, KOR and CHN. Decisions marked "Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-29" were taken without the author, under §1's rule.

| ID | Decision | Why | Status |

| B-346 | **Search and archiving routes for the Russian Federation.** (1) **No web search** (the run's allowance of 200 calls was used up, as B-331, B-336, B-341). (2) **mid.ru cannot be read or archived**: every scripted request is answered with an F5 bot-challenge page (JavaScript), and the Wayback Machine's captures of the Ministry's pages are the same 3 KB challenge pages, so no Ministry text could support an event; the Ministry's own site was not searched. The Foreign Minister's statements at the United Nations were taken from the Permanent Mission's site (russiaun.ru, part of the Ministry), whose full English list of statements (69 pages, 1,104 titles, June 2023 to September 2026) was read by title, and captured anew with Save Page Now (5 sources). (3) **en.kremlin.ru** answers scripted requests; its own search was run for 15 terms and the items of the window read for Gaza passages. Save Page Now answers the site's item URLs by resolving them to listing pages (the archiver recorded `/events/president/news/page/41` for an item), so the two such results were discarded (their `archive/index.csv` rows and texts removed before the commit, as B-303) and each Kremlin source was recorded from an existing Wayback capture of the item URL itself with `--capture` (B-188), checked to hold the item (title and date in the archived HTML): 5 sources, two of them from captures made on 2026-09-29. (4) government.ru (the Prime Minister) searched for Gaza and Palestine. (5) gadebate.un.org: the Russian PDFs of 2024 and 2026 have no usable text layer, and Save Page Now answered HTTP 405 for that of 2025 (no capture exists; its empty index row was removed): the Mission's English texts were used instead. (6) Russia had Comtrade rows from P-14 (mirror only, B-194), so no fetch was run. No leads (no press or NGO claim was read). The terms and pages are listed in the header of `data/assessments/RUS.yaml`. | docs/06 §4 and §6; the task's rule not to bypass limits. | Applied. Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-29. |

The President (head of state; the head of government is the Prime Minister, whose remarks read contain no Gaza call) is filed as a B9 speaker as France's and China's Presidents are (B-342 (2)). (2) **The names-violations tier (+5)** was applied, as for China's events of 21 November 2023 and 30 May 2024 (B-342 (3)), to statements that name a specific act against civilians in Gaza, whether or not the speaker calls it a violation: collective punishment or "collective responsibility" (the President, 30 October 2023; the Foreign Minister, 23 January, 17 July, 27 and 28 September 2024, 27 September 2025), indiscriminate killing (30 October 2023), the "mass expulsion of civilians" (21 November 2023), forcible relocation (23 January 2024), blockade and starvation (27 September 2024, 27 September 2025), and the "decisions to ban even humanitarian aid and supplies of essential items and electricity to the Gaza Strip" (10 May 2025). (3) Nine B9 events (8 at +5, 1 at +2); the Foreign Minister's Security Council statement of 27 September 2024 and General Assembly statement of 28 September 2024 are different days, hence two events. (5) **B5/B6 none-found**: Russia is not a party to the Rome Statute; no statement by the President, the Prime Minister or the Foreign Minister on the warrants of November 2024 was found in the sources that could be read. (6) No sanction, recall, agreement, embargo, divestment, medical-evacuation or visa measure found (A3, A5–A8, B3, B4, B7, B10–B12, C1, C2, C4–C6, D4, D5 none-found); the evacuation of Russian citizens and their families from Gaza (November 2023) is consular, not D4 or D5; E1–E3 unchecked. | docs/02 §2 precisions; the P-D prompt; symmetric treatment across countries. | Applied. The **Wayback Machine's CDX index** of the two sites was used as the search facility, read only: 4,254 URL keys of the Ministry's press notes (205 notes of 2023 to 2026 matching the terms listed in the header of `data/assessments/BRA.yaml` read by title, about 30 in full), the 329 captures of the Minister's speeches and articles, and 647 URL keys of the Presidency's speeches (every international speech of 2023 to 2026 read for Gaza passages, about 35 in full; no capture after July 2026). (3) Every one of the 15 sources was recorded from an existing Wayback capture with `--capture` (B-188): 12 speeches and statements, the joint statement of 30 April 2026, the Ministry's note of 21 September 2025 (the B8 row) and the ICJ press release on Brazil's declaration (UNISPAL copy, B-203). (4) The President's General Debate statement of **22 September 2026** was read in the built-in browser on gadebate.un.org (PDF text read with pdf.js, read only); Save Page Now answered HTTP 405 twice and no Wayback capture exists, so it is not filed and its empty `archive/index.csv` row was removed before the commit, as B-303. The ICJ's PDF of Brazil's **declaration of intervention** sits behind a bot check in scripts and in the built-in browser (not bypassed) and Wayback holds only HTTP 403 captures of it. (5) Brazil had Comtrade rows from P-14 (own and mirror), so no fetch was run. No leads (no press or NGO claim was read). (6) **B9 standard** as B-332, B-337, B-342, B-347: filed only on the speaker's own formal words (the President's speeches, the Minister's statements published in full in his name, and one joint statement of Ministers he agreed); **not filed: the Ministry's press notes issued in the name of "o governo brasileiro"**, unsigned government statements treated as the spokesperson statements of the other wave-6 countries, although many of them name violations (for instance the note of 22 August 2025 condemning "o uso da fome como método de guerra"); nor the President's press statements after bilateral meetings without a call or a named violation. ### P-D — Country research, wave 7: MEX (2026-09-29)

Branch `data/wave-7` (base and numbering as B-356), pull request #8 to `main` (not merged by the session). Decisions marked "Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-29" were taken without the author, under §1's rule.

| ID | Decision | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| B-366 | **Routes for Mexico and the B9 standard.** (1) The branch was checked out and pulled; `origin/data/structured` had no commit the branch lacked (ce00297), so no merge; the base row is B-356. (2) **No web search** (the run's allowance of 200 calls was used up, as for Colombia and Chile). **www.gob.mx** answers scripted requests with a JavaScript proof-of-work challenge page ("Challenge Validation"; no CAPTCHA is shown). It was read in the built-in browser, which runs the page's script as any visitor's browser does, and every source was then archived with `pnpm archive` through Save Page Now (whose crawler receives the real pages); this is read-only use of the browser as docs/06 §1 rule 8 allows, not the bypass of a CAPTCHA, and is flagged for the author. Later wave-8 sessions check the branch out, pull, and merge `origin/data/structured` in (merge commit) when it has commits the branch lacks. Numbering: B-202 to B-380 are on `data/wave-1` to `data/wave-7`, which this branch does not contain; it continues at B-381 and cites those rows as "on data/wave-N". Taken over byte for byte from `data/wave-7`: the test-harness fix of B-324 on `data/wave-5` (`packages/pipeline/src/build/index.test.ts`) and UNRWA's donor tables for 2022 and 2024 with their texts and index rows. `archive/index.csv` and this file will conflict at the later merges (append-only: keep both sides). The archived bytes are unchanged: only the text extraction is new. (3) **Routes**: the agency's public search API (`portalapi.spa.gov.sa/api/v1/news/search`, the site's own search) gave the English titles of 7 October 2023 to 28 September 2026 for 17 terms (12,135 items); the bodies of 1,530 items on Gaza, Palestine, Israel, the Courts or UNRWA and of the Crown Prince's speeches were fetched and searched by script, about 60 read in full (list in the header of `data/assessments/SAU.yaml`); www.mofa.gov.sa lists few items after 2023 (its statements are published by the agency). The first session had no web search (allowance used up); the second had one and used it for the gaps (A5, B5, B8, D4, E2) and two leads. ### P-D — Country research, wave 8: QAT (2026-09-29)

Branch `data/wave-8`, pull request to `main` (not merged by the session). Decisions marked "Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-29" were taken without the author, under §1's rule.

| ID | Decision | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| B-391 | **Routes for Qatar and the B9 standard.** (1) **Base**: the branch was checked out and pulled; `origin/data/structured` had no commit the branch lacked (ce00297), so no merge; the base row is B-381. (2) **Routes**: the Ministry of Foreign Affairs (mofa.gov.qa, Sitefinity) serves its pages as HTML and answers scripted requests; its site search (`/en/search?indexCatalogue=mofasite&searchQuery=…`, every page) was harvested for 20 terms (3,498 distinct items, 1,350 dated from 7 October 2023; titles and summaries read), and its lists "Prime Minister Speeches" and "Press Conferences" were read through the site's own listing API (`/api/DynamicItems`): every speech (16) and press conference (18) of the Prime Minister from October 2023 read in full, with about 40 news items. Older news addresses carry Hijri dates (`1445/…`); several of 2023 answer 404 today. The Amiri Diwan needs JavaScript; the Qatar News Agency was not read. A web search was used for the medical-evacuation programme, the State Department's transcripts and the joint statement of 26 October 2023. (3) **B9 standard**, as B-381 (4): the speaker's own words and joint statements of foreign ministers, citing Gaza. Sheikh Mohammed bin Abdulrahman bin Jassim Al Thani is Prime Minister and Minister of Foreign Affairs (since 7 March 2023), so one speaker holds both B9 roles; the Ministry publishes his speeches and press-conference remarks as first-person transcripts, which carry the filed events; its news items (indirect speech) only date them. The Amir, head of state, is not a B9 speaker (B-390 (2)). The Official Spokesperson (Adviser to the Prime Minister) and the Ministers of State are not B9 speakers. (2) **Routes**: the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, African Cooperation and Moroccan Expatriates (diplomatie.ma, Drupal, English and French) answers the first scripted request, then a JavaScript bot check ("Request Rejected" or a challenge page) that was not bypassed; its pages were read in the built-in browser, where the site's search gives only the five best matches per query. Candidate items were listed from the Wayback Machine's index of the site's English and French addresses containing gaza, palestin, israel, al-qods/al-quds, unrwa, cessez or ceasefire (about 175 addresses) and from web searches restricted to the site; about 15 items were read in full (list in the header of `data/assessments/MAR.yaml`). Fresh captures with `pnpm archive` succeeded (Save Page Now's crawler received the pages; one needed its third attempt). The UN general-debate site refused Save Page Now (HTTP 405); the Head of Government's Arabic statement of the 79th session was recorded from an existing capture (`--capture 20250618062411`), then its index row and text were removed unused (this session's, never cited) because its text layer is garbled (B-403 (3)). The Head of Government's site refuses scripted requests (registry note) and was not read. A web search was used for the General Assembly speeches, the field hospital, agreements with Israel, African Lion, the port calls, the liaison offices and protests. (3) **B9 standard**, as B-381 (4), B-386 (3) and B-397 (4): the speaker's own words (words the Ministry quotes directly) and joint statements of foreign ministers, citing Gaza. Nasser Bourita is Minister of Foreign Affairs and Aziz Akhannouch Head of Government throughout the window; King Mohammed VI, head of state, is not a B9 speaker (B-390 (2)), nor is the Director General who spoke for Morocco at the Paris conference of 9 November 2023. ### P-D — Country research, wave 9: IDN (2026-09-29)

Branch `data/wave-9`, pull request to `main` (not merged by the session). Decisions marked "Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-29" were taken without the author, under §1's rule.

| ID | Decision | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| B-407 | **Wave-9 base, numbering, routes for Indonesia and the extraction of content-API pages.** (1) **Base**: `data/wave-9` did not exist on origin, so it was created from `origin/data/structured` (ce00297; PR #1, the P-14 tables from which B1, B2, A1, A4, A2, C3, D1 and B8 are generated, still open) rather than from `main`, as wave 8 did (B-381 on `data/wave-8`); its pull request targets `main` and states that it depends on PR #1. Later wave-9 sessions check the branch out, pull, and merge `origin/data/structured` in (merge commit) when it has commits the branch lacks. Numbering: B-202 to B-406 are on `data/wave-1` to `data/wave-8`, which this branch does not contain; it continues at B-407 and cites those rows as "on data/wave-N". Taken over byte for byte from `data/wave-8`: the test-harness fix of B-324 on `data/wave-5` (`packages/pipeline/src/build/index.test.ts`), the `__NEXT_DATA__` extractor fallback of B-381 (2) (`extract.ts`, its test in `archive.test.ts`), and UNRWA's donor tables for 2022 and 2024 with their texts and index rows. `archive/index.csv`, `recognitions.csv` and this file will conflict at the later merges (append-only: keep both sides). (2) **Content-API pages**: the Ministry of Foreign Affairs' site (kemlu.go.id) is a client-rendered application whose pages carry no text in the served HTML (a Wayback capture of a page holds only the shell); each page loads its item from the Ministry's public content API (`backpanel.kemlu.go.id/public-content-service/api/content/publication?slug=…&portal=pusat`, JSON with the title, date and Indonesian and English text as HTML strings). The API URL of each item is archived with `pnpm archive` (Save Page Now captured the JSON), and `extractText` now reads a JSON document whose strings carry HTML markup as prose: its strings with a space, in document order, markup turned into text by the existing DOM walk, one line per paragraph (method `json-content`, `jsonContentText`; the walk is shared with the `next-data` fallback as `jsonProse`). JSON without markup (the FTS, Comtrade and World Bank datasets) stays raw, as before; unit test with a reduced API record and a dataset record. The archived bytes are unchanged: only the text extraction is new. The source record's `url` is the API URL; its notes say so. (3) **Routes**: the site's own listing API gave every item of the sections Berita (3,811), Siaran Pers (237), Pidato Menteri (222), Pidato Presiden (57) and Pidato Lainnya (38) with full texts; 467 items from 7 October 2023 mention Gaza, Palestine, Israel, UNRWA, Rafah, the Courts, Netanyahu, genocide or the Board of Peace and were searched by script per indicator, about 40 read in full (list in the header of `data/assessments/IDN.yaml`). The Cabinet Secretariat's search feed (setkab.go.id, November 2024 onward) listed the President's remarks; its search pages then timed out, and presidenri.go.id refuses scripted requests (HTTP 403). A web search was used for B5, B8 and D4 and the leads. (4) **B9 standard**, as B-381 (4) on `data/wave-8`: the speaker's own words in full texts, citing Gaza, one per occasion and not for further statements in the same weeks; not the Ministry's unsigned statements, the Vice Ministers' statements or indirect speech. Indonesia has a presidential system: the President is head of state and head of government and is a B9 speaker (unlike the monarchs of wave 8, B-390 (2) on `data/wave-8`). (5) **Comtrade**: `pnpm fetch:comtrade --reporters IDN` was run on 2026-09-29 and stopped before any trade query, Save Page Now refusing the capture of the Comtrade reporter list (`error:too-many-daily-captures`, as B-383 (3) on `data/wave-8`); the empty index row the failed run appended was removed (the run's own, never cited). | The generated indicators do not exist on `main` before PR #1 is merged; the task's base rule; docs/06 §1 (rules 2, 3, 8) and §6; docs/02 §2 (B9: "head of government or foreign minister"). | Applied. Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-29 ((1) base from `data/structured`; (2) the `json-content` extraction; (4) the President as B9 speaker). |
| B-408 | **Readings of the Indonesian evidence, flagged for the author.** (1) **Tiers**: six statements are +5 on the Foreign Minister's own words: Israel's evacuation orders for 22 hospitals in Gaza contrary to international humanitarian law and collective punishment forbidden (OIC, Jeddah, 18 October 2023); clear violations of international humanitarian law in Gaza and attacks on hospitals (General Assembly, 28 November 2023); Israel's campaign against civilians in Gaza and forcible displacement in violation of Article 49 of the Fourth Geneva Convention (ICJ advisory proceedings, 23 February 2024); the killing of more than 34,000 Palestinians by Israel called a genocide (OIC Summit, Banjul, 4 May 2024); the siege of Gaza as collective punishment and forced mass displacement (ICJ advisory proceedings, 30 April 2025); the clear violations of international law in Gaza and the West Bank (General Assembly, 26 September 2026). Statements in the ICJ's advisory proceedings are filed as B9 (formal statements with the Ministry's official text); they are not case 192 and have no B3 or B4 effect. (2) **The President's General Assembly address of 23 September 2025** is not filed: it speaks of suffering, genocide in general terms and starvation in Gaza but names no violation attributed to anyone and makes no ceasefire call; his remarks of 22 September 2025 at the two-state conference ("We must stop the humanitarian catastrophe in Gaza. Ending the war must be our utmost priority.") qualify at +2 but could not be archived ((4)). His statements that Indonesia would recognise Israel once Israel recognises Palestine and would support guarantees of Israel's security are not B10. (3) **The Security Council statement of 26 May 2026** is filed at +5 on "families erased in an instant, hospitals destroyed, and aid obstructed while famine looms" in Gaza, without an actor named in that sentence; a stricter reading (+2 would not apply either, as it has no ceasefire call) would drop it; with it B9 reaches its +10 cap (sum +13.4); without it B9 would be +8.4 and the score +26. (4) **Not archivable on 2026-09-29**: the Foreign Minister's statement at the OIC Council of Foreign Ministers, Jeddah, 7 March 2025 (Israel's blocking of aid to Gaza "a blatant violation of International Humanitarian Law", +5): Save Page Now accepted three captures (two URL forms of the API record) but the Wayback Machine did not serve them within the archiver's 300 s wait, and its CDX server answered "Temporarily Offline"; the Cabinet Secretariat's transcript of 22 September 2025: Save Page Now captured a bot-check page (5,511 bytes), whose text file and index row were removed. Neither is filed; the failed runs' empty index rows were removed. (5) **Dates**: the General Assembly statement of 28 November 2023 is dated from the Minister's press briefing of that day (its record's foot says 29 November); the Banjul statement is headed 4 May 2024 (the Summit sat on 4 and 5 May; published 5 May), so its source id was renamed from `src_20240505_…` to `src_20240504_…` (text file and index row renamed before filing; bytes and hash unchanged). ### P-D — Country research, wave 9: DZA (2026-09-29)

Branch `data/wave-9` (base per B-407; `origin/data/structured` had no commits the branch lacked, so no merge was needed), pull request #10 to `main` (not merged by the session). Decisions marked "Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-29" were taken without the author, under §1's rule.

| ID | Decision | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| B-417 | **Routes for Algeria.** (1) **Ministry of Foreign Affairs** (mfa.gov.dz): a Next.js site whose pages carry their content in the page data; its listings were read by script (English: News and press releases 187 of 196 pages, Video news, Statements, Announcements, Diplomacy; Arabic: the same sections except the news listing, nearly all pages; the script stopped when the site slowed under the load). The Arabic items carry the full texts of the Minister's and the President's statements, while most English items of the Video news section carry the video only; statements are therefore filed on the Arabic pages (`https://www.mfa.gov.dz/ar/…`), archived with `pnpm archive` (text by readability) and quoted in the original Arabic with an English translation beside it. Two parallel `pnpm archive` runs rewrote `archive/index.csv` at the same moment and one row was lost; it was restored from the run's own output and its hash checked against the Wayback copy (b423f671…); later captures ran one at a time. (2) **UN Official Document System**: the verbatim records S/PV.9534 (23 January 2024), S/PV.9841 (20 January 2025) and A/79/PV.17 (30 September 2024), where the Minister spoke in Arabic, archived as PDFs and quoted in the UN's English (the Ministry's site has the video only for the latter two; for 23 January 2024 the Ministry's Arabic text is the first evidence and the UN's English the second). The 80th session's general-debate records (A/80/PV.6 to 20) returned no PDF. (3) **Presidency** (el-mouradia.dz): no response to a direct request (timeout); its Wayback captures hold the application shell only; the President's speeches are taken from the Ministry's site. The listing has gaps: no post for June 2024 or September to December 2024, no press release between May 2024 and July 2025. The Wayback Machine's index of the site (6,882 URLs) was searched for the same terms. (2) **State House** (statehouse.gov.ng): WordPress; its REST API searched for the same terms; it holds only 16 posts for September to December 2024, and Wayback's index of the site for November 2023 to January 2025 has no page on the Arab-Islamic summits. (3) **UN documents**: A/79/PV.8 (the Vice-President's general-debate statement of 24 September 2024) from the Official Document System; gadebate.un.org answers with an AWS WAF CAPTCHA (not attempted, docs/06 §1 rule 8); Nigeria's national statement at the two-state conference of July 2025 (UNISPAL PDF) and PAL/2250 (read from its Wayback capture: delivered by "the representative of Nigeria"). ### P-17 — Design critique and anti-slop pass (2026-09-30)

Site and code on `main`, merged into `data/phase1` with a merge commit; this log, PROMPTS.md and two French summaries on `data/phase1`. The pages were looked at on a local preview build of `data/phase1` (B-461): the 773 reviewed events shown as if published, build date 2026-09-30; every number quoted here is provisional until the author publishes (P-22 re-runs the checks). The skills design:design-critique, design:accessibility-review and frontend-design were invoked; the brief (docs/05) fixes the visual direction, so no new direction was proposed. Decisions marked "Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-30" were taken without the author, under §1's rule.

| ID | Decision | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| B-461 | **A local preview build.** `pnpm build:data --preview` (packages/pipeline/src/build-cli.ts, tested) builds the `reviewed` events as if published, never `draft` ones, with `review.reviewed_by` "PREVIEW, not reviewed by the author" and `reviewed_at` the build date written in memory only (the validator requires both on a published event); nothing under `data/` is written, and the command prints a PREVIEW warning. The deploy workflows never pass it, and `apps/web/scripts/deploy-check.ts` now refuses an output whose API lists a hand-authored event that `data/` does not hold as published (tested). The site was then built from it in both modes: `pnpm turbo run build --filter=@gai/web...` (scorecard, out/) and `pnpm --filter @gai/web build:kit` (score mode, out-kit/, which also holds the kit). | The run's instruction (look at country pages with their events before the author's review; a local preview option only). | Applied (docs/04 §2 and §5). Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-30. |
| B-462 | **Method.** Every page of the prompt in EN and FR (home, ranking, the country pages of USA, DEU, KEN, VCT and ISR, Compare empty, with DEU and with USA, DEU, FRA, ZAF, BRA, Changes, the report of January 2024, methodology, data, about, corrections, reply, embed) at 360, 768 and 1280 px with JavaScript and at 360 and 1280 px without, `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` throughout, in both builds, the live site (https://gaza-accountability-index.pages.dev) and the preview of PR #11 (https://data-phase1.gaza-accountability-index.pages.dev): a Playwright script measured horizontal overflow and elements past the gutter, the fonts in use, text under 12 px, shadows, radii over 2 px, gradients other than the no-data hatching, hyphen-minus before a figure, French spacing before ; : ? ! » and after «, English text in French pages without `lang`, uppercase text, transitions over 150 ms and animations, filled buttons per view, targets under 24 px, overlapping SVG labels, console errors; screenshots of each view were read. The kit's /_kit/changes/ (filters, a report with movers, a new event and an end) was read at 1280 px. Ten share cards were opened (scorecard: DEU, fr/VCT, ISR, KEN; score: USA, fr/GBR, ZAF, fr/DEU, VCT, fr/PSE). The widget ran on a page of another origin (127.0.0.1:4199, Georgia and red text as host styles, no site font) at 320, 480 and 1280 px, both views, both modes, EN and FR. (2) score-mode ranking: under 1024 px the band chip sits under the score and the Band column is hidden, since the French table overflowed by 14 px at 360 px and 9 px at 768 px; (3) Compare: CategoryDots' screen-reader table was `sr-only` itself, and a table cannot be narrower than its content, so five countries widened a phone page by 179–197 px (score mode) — the wrapper is now the sr-only box; the scorecard counts table overflowed by 57 px (EN) and 197 px (FR) at 360 px and now scrolls in its own container; the chart's direct labels ran past the screen at 360 px ("United States of America −100") — the ISO3 code on phones and for names over 26 characters, the name otherwise, in a 13rem label column from 768 px; EventDiff columns keep 12rem when several countries are compared (the table scrolls rather than setting summaries three words to the line); (4) share cards: "−100" at 168 px ran the band chip into the number — a four-character score is set at 128 px, the row may use 32 px of the column's padding and the chip wraps under the score if both still do not fit; (5) ISO dates in the summary line broke after a hyphen ("2026-" / "05-28") on the country page and in the widget: kept whole (`NoBreakDates`, a `white-space: nowrap` span; the widget grows by 36 bytes gzipped);
