### P-19 — Security and code review (2026-09-30)

Code, workflows, dependencies and docs/04 on `main` (4583e63, 4d0885c, 626e843, 8564119, 74fd00d, pushed; CI and the deploy ran on them), merged into `data/phase1` with merge commits (c178cad, f47ec3f); this log and PROMPTS.md on `data/phase1`. No event, source, assessment, point, cap, threshold or rule changed; no score moved. The two review skills were invoked: `security-review` reviews a branch's diff, and `main` was clean, so its method and false-positive rules were applied by hand to the whole repository; `engineering:code-review` is a checklist (security, performance, correctness, maintainability), applied to the same scope and to this session's diff. The phase1-only code (the calibration CLI, the `next-data` and `json-content` extractors, the Comtrade batching, the SIPRI importer) was read for the same points. Issues opened for what was not fixed: #12, #13. Decisions marked "Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-30" were taken without the author, under §1's rule.

| ID | Decision | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| B-476 | **Headers.** `public/_headers` adds `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000` (no `includeSubDomains`, no `preload`) and `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin` to documents; both are removed from `/_next/static/*`, `/fonts/*` and `/embed/*` like the other page-level headers; `scripts/headers.test.ts` checks both. Checked live on 2026-09-30 after the deploy of 8564119: `/en/` carries both and the CSP, `/embed/v1/gai.js` neither. The header CSP keeps `'unsafe-inline'` for scripts and styles because every page's `<meta>` CSP narrows it to hashed elements and the build fails on a page without the meta (B-102); read again and kept. | P-19 prompt ("CSP and headers on the live site"). `pages.dev` is on the HSTS preload list already; the header is for a custom domain, whose owner decides the rest. | Applied. |
| B-477 | **GitHub Actions.** Every action pinned by commit SHA with its tag in a comment (`actions/checkout` 3d3c42e = v7.0.1, `pnpm/action-setup` 0977fd9 = v6.0.10, `actions/setup-node` 8207627 = v7.0.0: the commits the moving tags pointed at, so no behaviour changed); `persist-credentials: false` on every checkout (nothing pushes; the install and build steps no longer find the token of `nightly.yml`/`wayback.yml`, which has `issues: write`, in `.git/config`); `deploy.yml` deploys production only for `refs/heads/main` (before, a manual run on any branch uploaded that branch as production) and previews only for pull requests from branches of this repository; `issue.sh` matches only issues opened by `github-actions[bot]` (`--author app/github-actions`), so an issue someone else titles "Nightly build failed" is neither commented on nor closed. Permissions stay `contents: read` (plus `issues: write` where an issue is opened); the Cloudflare secrets reach only the upload step of pushes to `main`, the nightly cron and same-repository pull requests. Untrusted strings: none of the workflows is triggered by issues, comments or `pull_request_target`; `github.head_ref` reaches the shell only through `env`. | P-19 prompt (least privilege, pinned SHAs, issue-form abuse, which events get the secrets). | Applied. |
| B-478 | **The prompt's open choices.** (1) Cloudflare's `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` on every `pages.dev` response, pages included → does not matter: no cookie, no credential, nothing private, and every page is public. (2) The Compare page's runtime fetch trusts the country file's shape beyond `iso3` and `excluded` → no further client-side check: same origin, files built from validated data, React escapes every text. (3) The widget under Trusted Types → the fallback link is now built with DOM calls (`linkText`, `createElement`), so a host page that refuses `innerHTML` still gets the link; a named policy is issue #13. (4) `gai.js` caching → default revalidation kept; no Subresource Integrity hash published, since the script changes with every build and a pinned hash would break every embed at the next nightly; a host that wants integrity can serve its own copy. Default adopted in the unattended run of 2026-09-30. |
| B-479 | **Archiver and keys.** `fetchBytes` refuses a body over `MAX_DOWNLOAD_BYTES` (512 MiB; the largest archived document is the 352 MB UN Digital Library file, B-186), on `Content-Length` or while streaming, and Save Page Now downloads do not retry it (`maxBytes` option); pdfjs is called with `enableXfa: false` beside `disableFontFace` and `useSystemFonts: false`, and pdfjs 6 has no `isEvalSupported` option because it has no eval or `new Function` path at all (a test reads `legacy/build/pdf.mjs` and `pdf.worker.mjs` for them); new `extract.test.ts`: a page with scripts, `onload`, `onerror`, `javascript:` links and `srcdoc` runs nothing and fetches nothing, a damaged PDF falls back to raw text, the 200 KB text cap and the download limit hold. `pnpm check:wayback` requests only `https://web.archive.org/web/{14 digits}id_/…` URLs (anything else is reported, not requested; all 1,003 rows of `data/phase1` and 26 of `main` match) and calls curl with `--proto =https`, `--max-filesize` and `--url`, so no value from a data file can be read as a curl option. Keys: the three values in `.env` were searched in the whole history (`git log --all -S`, every branch) and the working tree (data, `archive/text/`, `.cache/`): no occurrence; a pattern scan of every commit for key-like assignments, `Authorization: LOW`, private-key blocks and token prefixes found only `.env.example`'s empty names; `.env` and `.cache/` were never tracked on any branch; the Save Page Now keys travel only in the `Authorization` header and the Comtrade key only in `Ocp-Apim-Subscription-Key` (phase1's batched Comtrade calls included); `.env` was readable by every local user and is now mode 600 (local only). | P-19 prompt (untrusted HTML/PDF, size limits, no key in a URL, log, record or archived text, `.env` and `.cache/` never tracked, `check:wayback` curl arguments). | Applied. |

Rule for the sessions after this one: check the byte count and the extracted text of every Beehive page before writing its record. (2) `gov.kz` (the Kazakh portal, with the Ministry of Foreign Affairs' releases) and `president.kg` load their texts by script: the HTML holds no text (836 bytes for a Ministry release) and the portal's API refuses scripted requests, so no Ministry release can be archived as text; the Ministry's release of 27 January 2026 on the memoranda with Israel is therefore known through two press reports (B-504 (2)). (3) `akorda.kz` answered Save Page Now with an internal server error: recorded from the Wayback capture of the day after. (4) `gadebate.un.org` statements are captured by Wayback at their file URLs `…/gastatements/{session}/{iso2}_{lang}.pdf` (`kg_ru`, `kg_en`, `nz_en`, `kz_en`): the 79th and 80th sessions were reached that way; the 81st (September 2026) has no capture yet. | B-509 | **Routes and lessons for the sessions after this one.** (1) `transcripts.un.org` (the UN's automatic transcript of a General Debate day) is a 1.9 MB page: `pnpm archive` truncates the extracted text at 200 KB, and the Tajik Foreign Minister's passage (26 September 2026, Day 5, timestamp 5:30:49) lies at about 224 KB, so no quotable copy exists; the record was removed (B-208) and the statement is `lead_20261005_TJK_1`. Filed from it, it would be a +2 event at full weight that ends Tajikistan's passivity penalty (+17 on the score). (2) Image-only PDFs: the General Debate PDFs of Turkmenistan (80th session) and Tajikistan (79th session) have no text layer (the archiver would extract garbage); they were read by OCR (`tesseract` after `pdftoppm`) to find out whether the speech mentions Gaza, and a text copy was sought (Tajikistan 2024: the Ministry's page prints the speech, archived; Turkmenistan 2025: no passage on Gaza, nothing archived). (3) `president.uz` serves the full English texts of the President's addresses as plain HTML (archived with Save Page Now, readable); `president.tj` is a script-loaded page and cannot be read or archived as text (as `president.kg`, B-501). (4) `gadebate.un.org` answers HTTP 405 to scripted requests, and Wayback has no capture of the 81st session's statements: the statements of September 2026 are read through press or the UN transcript only. (5) `embassies.gov.il` (Israeli Government pages) accepts Save Page Now; `azertag.az` answers 403 (the failed record was removed); Wayback's CDX answered 429 after a burst of queries (waited). | docs/06 §1 (rule 8), §6; B-208, B-501. | Applied; (1) scheduled (P-23). |

### T-P21-04 — P-21, light protocol: BLR, MDA, ROU (2026-10-05, unattended run)

Light protocol (docs/06 §5) for Belarus, the Republic of Moldova and Romania, on `data/phase1`: 15 hand-written events at status `reviewed` (0, 4, 11), 3 assessment files, 3 lead files (7 leads), 15 source records (8 for Romania, 7 for Moldova, none for Belarus), `gov_sources` for the three, no code change. Nothing is published and no `review.reviewed_by` is set. The summary table and the per-country counts are in `docs/review/light-protocol.md`.

| ID | Decision | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| B-523 | **Routes and lessons for the sessions after this one.** (1) `gadebate.un.org` answers a scripted request with HTTP 405 only when it carries no browser User-Agent: a request with an ordinary browser `User-Agent` returns the PDF (200) for every session, the 81st included (read on 2026-10-05); Save Page Now still fails on the site (error `method-not-allowed`), so a General Debate file with no Wayback capture (the 80th and 81st sessions) can be read but not archived, and a statement found only there is a lead (as B-509 (1) for a UN transcript). `docs.un.org` and `documents.un.org` serve the verbatim records (A/80/PV.12, PV.13) through a script-loaded viewer and an error page to a script. (2) `mae.ro` (Romania's Ministry of Foreign Affairs), its New York mission `mpnewyork.mae.ro` and `presidency.ro` answer every request with a JavaScript bot check (HTTP 503, 'Verifying your browser'), Save Page Now included (error `service-unavailable`) and their Wayback captures of 2026 are the same check page (8,904 bytes): no Ministry release can be archived; the releases are known through the outlets that quote them (Mediafax, Digi24, HotNews, Agerpres), and a Ministry item is a lead unless a good capture exists. (3) Pages that accept Save Page Now and read well: `ms.ro` (Ministry of Health), `igsu.ro` (Inspectorate for Emergency Situations), `mfa.gov.md`, `agerpres.ro`, `mediafax.ro`, `digi24.ro`, `hotnews.ro`, `radiomoldova.md`, `radiochisinau.md`, `moldpres.md`, `moldova.europalibera.org`, `israel.mfa.gov.md`; pages that refuse it (HTTP 403): `gov.il` (the Israeli ministries' pages), `aninews.in`, `noi.md` (Cloudflare checks): four failed records (their `archive/index.csv` rows, never a source file) were removed before the commit (B-208). (4) The Moldovan Embassy to Israel's page `israel.mfa.gov.md/ro/content/cadrul-juridic-bilateral` lists every intergovernmental agreement and interinstitutional document with Israel with its date of signature: an official primary for C2 (the dates decided four events); the same kind of page may exist for other missions (a country's bilateral-treaty list is the first place to look for C2 and D5). (5) `gov.md` pages of 2024 now answer "Resource not found": the Wayback capture of 15 November 2024 was recorded with `--capture`. (6) `web.archive.org` answers HTTP 429 (and its CDX and availability endpoints return empty or 429) for minutes after a burst: space the requests; a Wayback capture of a General Debate file is found by requesting `web.archive.org/web/2id_/{url}` and reading the redirect's timestamp. | docs/06 §1 (rule 8), §6; B-188, B-208, B-501, B-509, B-515. | Applied; rule for T-P21-05 to T-P21-49. |
| B-524 | **`gov_sources`.** Belarus: the Ministry (`mfa.gov.by/en/`, 200; the English edition carries no lang attribute, recorded `en`), the President (`president.gov.by/en`, 200, `en`) and the Council of Ministers (`government.by/en`: no answer from the session's network, connection failure; the Wayback capture of 2026-05-12 declares `en`). Moldova: the Ministry (`mfa.gov.md/en`, `en`), the Presidency (`presedinte.md/eng`, `en`) and the Government (`gov.md/en`, `en`), all 200. Romania: the Government (`gov.ro/en`, 200, no lang attribute, the English edition, `en`); the Ministry (`www.mae.ro/en`) and the Presidency (`www.presidency.ro/en`) answer with the bot check of B-523 (2), so their declared language could not be read: the Ministry's root is recorded `ro` (search-engine results show its English items under `/en/node/` and its Romanian items under `/node/`) and the Presidency's English edition `en`, each said so in the entry's notes. | The P-21 prompt; B-516. | Applied. |
| B-525 | **Readings flagged for the author (Romania).** (1) **D2** (`evt_2024_01_29_ROU_D2`): the Ministry's release of 29 January 2024 ('Until the investigation is finalised, the Ministry will not start procedures for new voluntary contributions to UNRWA') is filed as a D2 hold under R10, although the Ministry's representatives said Romania had announced no voluntary commitment for 2024, so the hold is narrower than other donors' freezes; `corroborated` on three Romanian outlets because the Ministry's page cannot be archived (B-523 (2)); drop it if the author reads 'no new procedure' as no hold. The D3 increased of 2023 (USD 270,755 against 210,748) ends on that day (B-445). (2) **The end of D2 and D3 restored** (`evt_2024_07_19_ROU_D3`, `reported`): no Romanian announcement of the resumption is archivable; the earliest dated archived document that names Romania among the partners that restored funding is the UK government's release of 19 July 2024, so D2 ends and D3 restored starts that day ('no later than'), and UNRWA's table for 2024 shows USD 42,194 received; the Ministry's item of 17 October 2024 (a humanitarian contribution for UNRWA operations in Jordan, as search excerpts read) is a lead (`lead_20261005_ROU_3`). D3 restored is +5 at weight 0.4 (+2.0 today); drop it, or raise it once a Romanian document is archived. (3) **D4** (`evt_2024_09_30_ROU_D4`): one event for the programme, dated from the Ministry of Health's release of 1 October 2024 on the arrival of 30 September 2024, with the IGSU release of 10 April 2025 as evidence of continuation; the Ministry of Foreign Affairs counted twelve flights by 31 August 2026 (its releases cannot be archived): `lead_20261005_ROU_4`. A reader who counts each documented phase as an event (R11) would add a D4 event of 31 August 2026 (+5, inside 365 days, a qualifying event that would end the passivity penalty without the EU listing). (4) **C2** (`evt_2024_05_12_ROU_C2`, `reported`): the memorandum of understanding on emergency wheat supplies signed in Bucharest on 12 May 2024 by Israel's Minister of Agriculture, from Agerpres relaying the TPS agency; Israel's page ('Treat the wheat', a 'declaration of intent' with Romania) answers 403; the Romanian signatory is not named; −10 at weight 0.4 (−4.0, standing, with no decay); drop it if the author excludes declarations of intent or single-origin press. (5) **B9 of 25 September 2024** (`evt_2024_09_25_ROU_B9`, the President): 'The end of hostilities, a safe release of all hostages and facilitation of the safe shipment of the humanitarian aid for the civilians in Gaza are imperatives no one can deny', filed at the call tier (+2) although worded as an imperative, as Bulgaria's (B-517 (1)); decayed to zero. (6) **B9 of 6 June 2024** (`evt_2024_06_06_ROU_B9`): the leaders' statement was signed for Romania by the President (Digi24, relaying the Presidential Administration), not the Prime Minister: the rule of B-444 (the head of government where the text names states only) is applied with the press report as the evidence of the signatory (as for Poland's President); decayed to zero. (7) **Not filed**: the Foreign Minister's General Debate statement of 27 September 2025 ('An immediate ceasefire in Gaza, the unconditional release of all hostages, as well as adequate humanitarian assistance are merely the first steps towards a permanent end to hostilities'), a +2 B9 inside the decay window, because the UN's file cannot be archived (B-523 (1)): `lead_20261005_ROU_1`; it would not be a qualifying event (weighted below 2). ### T-P21-05 — P-21, light protocol: SVK, UKR, ATG (2026-10-06, unattended run)

Light protocol (docs/06 §5) for Slovakia, Ukraine and Antigua and Barbuda, on `data/phase1`: 11 hand-written events at status `reviewed` (9, 0, 2), 3 assessment files, 3 lead files (10 leads), 6 source records (2 each), `gov_sources` for the three, no code change. Nothing is published and no `review.reviewed_by` is set. The summary table and the per-country counts are in `docs/review/light-protocol.md`.

| ID | Decision | Why | Status |

| B-532 | **Routes and lessons for the sessions after this one.** (1) `mzv.sk` (Slovakia's Ministry of Foreign and European Affairs) answers every scripted request with HTTP 403 (WebFetch included); Wayback holds a few of its pages (the statements page of 2025-03-26, which lists titles only; the home page of 2025-05-28; one release, of 17 February 2026, captured 2026-04-11 and recorded with `--capture`) and none of most releases that search engines list; its releases are known through TASR, SITA and the outlets that relay them (`teraz.sk`, `sita.sk`, `netky.sk`, `pravda.sk`, `info.sk` all answer a script), in indirect speech, which R4 does not accept for B9 or B10. `prezident.sk` and `vlada.gov.sk` answer 200. (2) Ukraine: `president.gov.ua` and `mfa.gov.ua` answer HTTP 403 and their Wayback captures are the 403 page (Save Page Now fails with `error:no-request`, so a failed record had to be removed from `archive/index.csv`, B-208); `kmu.gov.ua` answers a CAPTCHA or a bot check but its English home page has a good capture; `ukrinform.net`, `kyivindependent.com` and `iiss.org` accept Save Page Now. The IISS Shangri-La Dialogue publishes its sessions' question-and-answer parts 'as delivered' (the only verbatim record of a head of state's answer there), but the validator (`event.statement-requirements`) requires a B9 event to cite a source of kind `official` or `official-video`, so a transcript of a think tank is a lead (B-534). (3) General Debate files: a delegation may re-upload under another name (`sk_en_0.pdf` for Slovakia's 80th session, `ag_en_0.pdf` for Antigua and Barbuda's 81st); the country's page `gadebate.un.org/en/{session}/{country-name}` (200 with a browser User-Agent) names the file; `81/ag_en.pdf` is byte-identical to `80/ag_en.pdf` (same MD5), so a statement is attributed to a session only from the page; the 79th and 80th sessions' files have Wayback captures (`web.archive.org/web/2id_/…` gives the timestamp; `--capture` records them with a readable text), the 81st's have none. An image-only file (Slovakia's 79th: the President's office's draft, headed 'Podklady pre PSR') and a file whose font encoding yields no text (Ukraine's 80th) are read by OCR (`pdftoppm -r 130` and `tesseract`); a statement read only so is not archived as a source (no text copy, B-509). (4) `press.un.org` pages answer a 3 KB script shell in captures of 2026 but captures of 2024 and 2025 hold the full text (request the timestamp `20250101`). (5) A transport or evacuation programme of a country may be dated from a receiving state's release already archived for another country (Ireland's release of 20 December 2024 names the plane of Slovakia's Government): look for the partner's release before the home ministry's. ## 3. Changes to PROMPTS.md since the plan

Every change adds or specifies; none removes or loosens a requirement. By prompt:

| Prompt | Added (by which session) |
|---|---|
| P-05 | Step 3 generates events with `generateAll`, checks each against the Event schema and the tone lint (a failure fails the build), writes generator notes to `build-notes.json`, and derives the assessment status of the generated indicators from the tables (P-04). |
